For several weeks she rode a wave of unusually intense productivity, staying up late tweaking spreadsheets and sketching plans for a side business offering bookkeeping to local Latino entrepreneurs, while still trying to keep up with salsa classes and weekend hikes. As the rush ended, she abruptly found herself unable to maintain her usual meticulous routines at work; errors began appearing in ledgers, she stopped going to art workshops, and her normally detailed travel and study plans felt overwhelming rather than exciting. Her supervisor’s concern about recent mistakes, combined with her own distress at how uncharacteristically disengaged she felt from both work and the community events she usually loved, led her to seek a mental health evaluation after about a month of noticeable change.\n- Chief Complaint: “Over the last month I’ve just lost interest in my art, dancing, and even my job, and I can’t seem to care about anything the way I used to.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Lately I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like something bad happened but I it’s like someone flipped a switch and all the things that used to light me up—my painting nights, salsa, even getting a clean set of books balanced—just feel flat and pointless, so I end up sitting even though things have slowed down, most days I have to force myself to eat half a sandwich at my desk and my pants are getting loose.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I toss and turn for beat, which is the total opposite of how fast I was flying through work a few weeks ago.\n- S029 (Fatigue): Even on days when I go to bed early, I drag myself around the office like my body weighs twice as much, and by the time I get home I’m so wiped out that doing the dishes or taking a shower feels like a big chore.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): When I see the corrections my supervisor has to make on my work, I just think, 'You’re useless, you can’t even keep numbers straight,' and I replay every little error in my head on repeat, feeling like I’ve let everyone down and shouldn’t have asked for more responsibility in the first place.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Lately I’ll read the same email three or four times and still can’t hold the details in my head, and even deciding something simple like which bill to pay first or what to cook for dinner can leave me just staring at the options, unable to pick.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): Right before this crash, I went through a couple of weeks where I felt almost buzzed all the time—super upbeat and a little snappy if anyone tried to slow me down—like I was on top of everything and people who couldn’t keep up were just annoying me.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): During that busy stretch I was taking on extra clients, reorganizing our filing system, drafting a full business plan for my own side gig, and still insisting on going to every salsa class and meetup, like I had endless energy and couldn’t sit still.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): When I was in that high phase, I honestly believed I was the only one in the office who really understood the clients’ finances and that I could easily build a firm of my own if I just pushed a little harder, even though I haven’t even started my CPA classes yet.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): For a few weeks during tax season I was sleeping maybe three or four hours a night, getting up at five to work on spreadsheets or sketch out marketing ideas, and I didn’t feel tired at all—more like sleep was just getting in the way.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): Around that time my coworkers joked that I’d had too much coffee because I would talk a mile a minute about new systems and business ideas, jumping from one topic to the next without really letting anyone get a word in.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): Back when I was riding that wave, my thoughts felt like they were (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from my normal workload to staying late to redo everyone’s spreadsheets, reaching out to old contacts about my future bookkeeping business, signing up for extra community events, and even planning weekend hikes and art projects all at once, like I had to keep doing something every minute.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: In the last month I’ve had to be pulled into meetings about my accuracy because I’m missing things I never used to, I’ve skipped several salsa classes and community events I’m usually reliable for, and at home I’m letting dishes pile up and living out of laundry baskets because I just can’t keep up.\n- duration: Looking back, these swings between feeling overly revved up and then sinking into these long, heavy lows have been happening off and on for about five months now, ever since the last tax season ramped up.\n- depressive_episode_duration: This current depressed stretch, where I feel flat, about anything the way I used to.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Lately I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like something bad happened but I asked for more responsibility in the first place.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Lately I’ll read the same email three or four times and still can’t hold the details in my head, and even deciding just annoying me.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): During that busy stretch I was taking on extra clients, reorganizing our filing system, drafting a full business plan for my own side gig, and still insisting on going to every salsa class and meetup, like I had endless energy and couldn’t sit still.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): When I was in that high phase, I honestly believed I was the only seek a mental health evaluation after about a month of noticeable change.\n- Chief Complaint: “Over the last month I’ve just lost interest in my art, dancing, and even my job, and I can’t seem to care about anything the way I used to.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Lately I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like something bad happened but I asked for more responsibility in the first place.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Lately I’ll read the same email three or four times and still can’t hold the details in my head, and even deciding beat, which is the total opposite of how fast I was flying through work a few weeks ago.\n- S029 (Fatigue): Even on days when I go to bed early, I drag myself around the office like my body weighs twice as much, and by the time I get home I’m so wiped out that doing the dishes or taking a shower feels like a big chore.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): When I see the corrections my supervisor has to make on my work, I just think, 'You’re useless, you can’t even keep numbers straight,' and I replay every little error in my head on repeat, feeling like I’ve let everyone down and shouldn’t have asked for more responsibility in the first place.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Lately I’ll read the same email three or four times and still can’t hold the details in my head, and even deciding something simple like which bill to pay first or what to cook for dinner can leave me just staring at the options, unable to pick.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): Right before this crash, I went through a couple of weeks where I felt almost buzzed all the time—super upbeat and a little snappy if anyone tried to slow me down—like I was on top of everything and people who couldn’t keep up were just annoying me.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): During that busy stretch I was taking on extra clients, reorganizing our filing system, drafting a full business plan for my own side gig, and still insisting on going to every salsa class and meetup, like I had endless energy and couldn’t sit still.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): When I was in that high phase, I honestly believed I was the only one in the office who really understood the clients’ finances and that I could easily build a firm of my own if I just pushed a little harder, even though I haven’t even started my CPA classes yet.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): For a few weeks during tax season I was sleeping maybe three or four hours a night, getting up at five to work on spreadsheets or sketch out marketing ideas, and I didn’t feel tired at all—more like sleep was just getting in the way.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): Around that time my coworkers joked that I’d had too much coffee because I would talk a mile a minute about new systems and business ideas, jumping from one topic to the next without really letting anyone get a word in.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): Back when I was riding that wave, my thoughts felt like they were all firing at once—new ways to help Latino business owners, tweaks to our billing process, art ideas—and I couldn’t slow them down enough to focus on just one thing at a time.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from my normal workload to staying late to redo everyone’s spreadsheets, reaching out to old contacts about my future bookkeeping business, signing up for extra community events, and even planning weekend hikes and art projects all at once, like I had to keep doing something every minute.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: In the last month I’ve had to be pulled into meetings about my accuracy because I’m missing things I never used to, I’ve skipped several salsa classes and community events I’m usually reliable for, and at home I’m letting dishes pile up and living out of laundry baskets because I just can’t keep up.\n- duration: Looking back, these swings between feeling overly revved up and then sinking into these long, heavy lows have been happening off and on for about five months now, ever since the last tax season ramped up.\n- depressive_episode_duration: This current depressed stretch, where I feel flat, exhausted, and guilty about everything, has been going on steadily for about five months since not long after that intense tax rush started.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only Even though my thoughts were racing and I felt incredibly confident—like I was fully capable of running my own business right then—I always knew what was real.\n\nDoctor: During these periods when you feel low and lose interest, do you find that you are still able to function at work and in your daily life, or do your symptoms make it impossible to carry out your normal responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Honestly, it's been really hard to keep up with anything, and I feel so embarrassed about it. I've been pulled into meetings at work because I'm making mistakes in the ledgers, and I've completely stopped going to my salsa classes and community events. At home, everything is piling up—I'm living out of laundry baskets and the dishes are just sitting in the sink because I'm too exhausted to care.\n\nDoctor: During these low periods, have you had any thoughts that life is not worth living, or have you felt like you are unable to function at all in your daily life?"

Her supervisor’s concern about recent mistakes, combined with her own distress at how uncharacteristically disengaged she felt from both work and the community events she usually loved, led her to seek a mental health evaluation after about a month of noticeable change.\n- Chief Complaint: “Over the last month I’ve just lost interest in my art, dancing, and even my job, and I can’t seem to care about anything the way I used to.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Lately I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like something bad happened but I beat, which is the total opposite of how fast I was flying through work a few weeks ago.\n- S029 (Fatigue): Even on days when I go to bed early, I drag myself around the office like my body weighs twice as much, and by the time I get home I’m so wiped out that doing the dishes or taking a shower feels like a big chore.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): When I see the corrections my supervisor has to make on my work, I just think, 'You’re useless, you can’t even keep numbers straight,' and I replay every little error in my head on repeat, feeling like I’ve let everyone down and shouldn’t have asked for more responsibility in the first place.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Lately I’ll read the same email three or four times and still can’t hold the details in my head, and even deciding something simple like which bill to pay first or what to cook for dinner can leave me just staring at the options, unable to pick.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): Right before this crash, I went Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor. Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy. Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences. Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nNo, I haven't been hospitalized and I am still going to work, but my functioning is definitely impaired. I've been making mistakes that led to meetings about my accuracy, and I'm falling behind on chores at home and skipping social events. But I haven't been completely unable to work or anything like that.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"
